| **P4 - `deleteContainersDirect` skips symlink cleanup on `NotFound`, leaving the exact leak this PR fixes in a race window** | [core/destroy.go:725-731](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L725-L731) |

## P4 - `deleteContainersDirect` skips symlink cleanup on `NotFound`, leaving the exact leak this PR fixes in a race window

**Location:** [core/destroy.go:725-731](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L725-L731)

**Problem:** The new code only calls `clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(name)` when `rt.DeleteContainer` succeeds. Both `DockerRuntime.DeleteContainer` &#40;[`runtime/docker/docker.go:1748-1754`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/runtime/docker/docker.go#L1748-L1754)&#41; and `PodmanRuntime.DeleteContainer` &#40;[`runtime/podman/podman.go:605-610`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/runtime/podman/podman.go#L605-L610)&#41; propagate a not-found error when the container is already gone, so in that case the added `continue` skips symlink removal and the stale `/run/netns/<name>` symlink — precisely what this PR sets out to clean — survives. This also diverges from the node-based path, where the symlink loop at [`core/destroy.go:466-471`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L466-L471) removes symlinks regardless of `node.Delete` errors.

**Trigger:** A container vanishes between `ListContainers` and `DeleteContainer`, for example a concurrent `destroy` &#40;including `destroy --all` racing itself&#41; or an operator removing the container manually. `DockerRuntime.produceGenericContainerList` already documents this list-to-delete race at [`runtime/docker/docker.go:1562-1564`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/runtime/docker/docker.go#L1562-L1564).

**Impact:** Minor&#58; one stale symlink per raced container. It self-heals when a same-named container is redeployed because `LinkContainerNS` removes an existing destination before symlinking &#40;[`utils/netlink.go:60-63`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/utils/netlink.go#L60-L63)&#41;, and `destroy` already logs the removal error. No silent success is claimed beyond the pre-existing behavior.

**Suggested correction:** Either attempt symlink removal after every delete attempt in `deleteContainersDirect` to mirror the node-based path, or keep the guard but let a not-found error fall through to symlink removal. Given the narrow race and the self-healing outcome, this is an optional improvement rather than a blocker.


## Coverage

- Both changed paths were fully traced&#58; the destroy-by-name-only flow reaching `deleteContainersDirect` with empty `c.Nodes` via `WithLabNameOnly`/`makeCopyForDestroy`, and the `orphanSidecars` call site when nodes exist.
- Symlink naming verified end to end&#58; created with the node `LongName` in docker and podman `postStartActions`, deleted with the trimmed `GenericContainer.Names[0]` from this PR; sibling patterns in `deleteApplyNodes` &#40;[`core/destroy.go:616`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L616-L616)&#41; and the node loop &#40;[`core/destroy.go:466-471`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L466-L471)&#41; were compared.
- The Robot test additions were checked against the keywords in [`tests/15-mgmt/mgmt.resource`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/tests/15-mgmt/mgmt.resource#L1-L1) and the fixture [`13-destroy-without-topo.clab.yml`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/tests/15-mgmt/13-destroy-without-topo.clab.yml#L1-L1) &#40;linux nodes `n1`/`n2` matching the asserted long names&#41;.
- Pre-existing gaps not worsened by this PR were noted but not raised as findings&#58; multi-netns node kinds &#40;e.g. SR OS component/netns/parking entries in [`nodes/sros/netns.go:139-156`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/nodes/sros/netns.go#L139-L156)&#41; still leave extra `/run/netns` entries in the name-only path, and `deleteToolContainers` never removed symlinks.
- Source-only review of the snapshot; no tests or builds were executed. The mgmt Robot suite is the executable check for this change and is wired into CI.
- No schema impact&#58; no topology or configuration API surface changed, and no user-facing docs describe `/run/netns` cleanup behavior.

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

        "type": "file",
        "path": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc",
        "text": "---\ndescription: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Karpathy behavioral guidelines\n\nBehavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",
        "lineStart": 1,
        "lineEnd": 75,
        "totalLines": 75,

      }

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

654: func (c *CLab) deleteNodes(ctx context.Context, workers uint) {
655: 	wg := new(sync.WaitGroup)
656: 
657: 	concurrentChan := make(chan clabnodes.Node)
658: 
659: 	workerFunc := func(i uint, input chan clabnodes.Node, wg *sync.WaitGroup) {
660: 		defer wg.Done()
661: 
662: 		for {
663: 			select {
664: 			case n := <-input:
665: 				if n == nil {
666: 					log.Debugf("Worker %d terminating...", i)
667: 					return
668: 				}
669: 
670: 				err := n.Delete(ctx)
671: 				if err != nil {
672: 					log.Errorf("could not remove container %q: %v", n.Config().LongName, err)
673: 				}
674: 			case <-ctx.Done():
675: 				return
676: 			}
677: 		}
678: 	}
679: 
680: 	// start concurrent workers
681: 	wg.Add(int(workers))
682: 
683: 	for i := range workers {
684: 		go workerFunc(i, concurrentChan, wg)
685: 	}
686: 
687: 	// send nodes to workers
688: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
689: 		concurrentChan <- n
690: 	}
691: 
692: 	// close channel to terminate the workers
693: 	close(concurrentChan)
694: 
695: 	// also call delete on the special nodes
696: 	for _, n := range c.getSpecialLinkNodes() {
697: 		err := n.Delete(ctx)
698: 		if err != nil {
699: 			log.Warn(err)
700: 		}
701: 	}
702: 
703: 	wg.Wait()
704: }
705: 
706: // deleteContainersDirect deletes containers directly via the runtime API.
707: // This is used when we don't have node objects (e.g., destroy-by-name-only case).
708: func (c *CLab) deleteContainersDirect(
709: 	ctx context.Context,
710: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
711: ) {
712: 	for _, cont := range containers {
713: 		if len(cont.Names) == 0 {
714: 			log.Warnf("skipping container %s with no names", cont.ID)
715: 			continue
716: 		}
717: 		name := cont.Names[0]
718: 		log.Infof("Removing container: %s", name)
719: 
720: 		c.logoutTailscaleContainer(ctx, cont)
721: 		rt := cont.Runtime
722: 		if rt == nil {
723: 			rt = c.globalRuntime()
724: 		}
725: 		err := rt.DeleteContainer(ctx, name)
726: 		if err != nil {
727: 			log.Errorf("could not remove container %q: %v", name, err)
728: 			continue
729: 		}
730: 
731: 		_ = clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(name)
732: 	}
733: }

735: func (c *CLab) deleteToolContainers(ctx context.Context) {
736: 	toolTypes := []string{"sshx", "gotty"}
737: 
738: 	if len(c.nodeFilter) == 0 {
739: 		toolTypes = append(toolTypes, clabnodestailscale.ToolType)
740: 	}
741: 
742: 	for _, toolType := range toolTypes {
743: 		toolFilter := []*clabtypes.GenericFilter{
744: 			{
745: 				FilterType: "label",
746: 				Field:      clabconstants.ToolType,
747: 				Operator:   "=",
748: 				Match:      toolType,
749: 			},
750: 			{
751: 				FilterType: "label",
752: 				Field:      clabconstants.Containerlab,
753: 				Operator:   "=",
754: 				Match:      c.Config.Name,
755: 			},
756: 		}
757:

1: package utils
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"path/filepath"
6: 	"strconv"
7: 	"strings"
8: )
9: 
10: // GetCanonicalImageName produces a canonical image name.
11: // if the input name did not specify a tag, the implicit "latest" tag is returned.
12: func GetCanonicalImageName(imageName string) string {
13: 	// name transformation rules
14: 	//    alpine == docker.io/library/alpine:latest
15: 	//    foo/bar == docker.io/foo/bar:latest
16: 	//    foo.bar/baz == foo.bar/bar:latest
17: 	//    localhost/foo:bar == localhost/foo:bar
18: 	//    myregistry:5000/foo == myregistry:5000/foo:latest
19: 	// docker.elastic.co/elasticsearch/elasticsearch ==
20: 	// docker.elastic.co/elasticsearch/elasticsearch:latest
21: 	canonicalImageName := imageName
22: 	slashCount := strings.Count(imageName, "/")
23: 
24: 	switch slashCount {
25: 	case 0:
26: 		canonicalImageName = "docker.io/library/" + imageName
27: 	case 1:
28: 		// split on slash to get first element of the name
29: 		nameSplit := strings.Split(imageName, "/")
30: 		// case of foo.bar/baz
31: 
32: 		switch {
33: 		case strings.Contains(nameSplit[0], "."):
34: 			canonicalImageName = imageName
35: 		case strings.Contains(nameSplit[0], ":"):
36: 			// case of myregistry:5000/foo - a registry addressed by host and port
37: 			canonicalImageName = imageName
38: 		case strings.Contains(nameSplit[0], "localhost"):
39: 			// case of localhost/foo:bar - podman prefixes local images with "localhost"
40: 			canonicalImageName = imageName
41: 		default:
42: 			canonicalImageName = "docker.io/" + imageName
43: 		}
44: 	}
45: 
46: 	// append latest tag if no tag was provided.
47: 	// only the last path element can carry a tag, a colon before it belongs
48: 	// to the registry port.
49: 	lastElement := canonicalImageName[strings.LastIndex(canonicalImageName, "/")+1:]
50: 	if !strings.Contains(lastElement, ":") {
51: 		canonicalImageName += ":latest"
52: 	}
53: 
54: 	return canonicalImageName
55: }
56: 
57: // ContainerNSToPID resolves the name of a container via
58: // the "/run/netns/<CONTAINERNAME>" to its PID.
59: func ContainerNSToPID(cID string) (int, error) {
60: 	pnns, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks("/run/netns/" + cID)
61: 	if err != nil {
62: 		return 0, err
63: 	}
64: 	pathElem := strings.Split(pnns, "/")
65: 	if len(pathElem) != 4 {
66: 		return 0, fmt.Errorf("unexpected result looking up container PID")
67: 	}
68: 	pid, err := strconv.Atoi(pathElem[1])
69: 	if err != nil {
70: 		return 0, fmt.Errorf("error converting the string part of the namespace link to int")
71: 	}
72: 	return pid, nil
73: }
74: 
75: // DestinationBindMountExists checks if a bind mount destination exists in a list of bind mounts.
76: // The bind options are not matched, only the destination is matched.
77: // The binds are expected to be in the format of "source:destination[:options]".
78: func DestinationBindMountExists(binds []string, dest string) bool {
79: 	for _, b := range binds {
80: 		parts := strings.Split(b, ":")
81: 		if len(parts) >= 2 {
82: 			// The destination is the second part
83: 			if parts[1] == dest {
84: 				return true
85: 			}
86: 		}
87: 	}
88: 	return false
89: }
90: 
91: // ContainerNameFromNetworkMode takes the NetworkMode config string and returns the container name
92: // from it.
93: func ContainerNameFromNetworkMode(s string) (string, error) {
94: 	after, found := strings.CutPrefix(s, "container:")
95: 	if !found {
96: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("%s not a valid container reference", s)
97: 	}
98: 	return after, nil
99: }

27: 
28: func (c *CLab) Destroy(ctx context.Context, options ...DestroyOption) (err error) {
29: 	opts := NewDestroyOptions()
30: 
31: 	for _, opt := range options {
32: 		opt(opts)
33: 	}
34: 
35: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
36: 		return c.destroyWithLabRuntime(ctx, opts)
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
40: 
41: 	switch {
42: 	case opts.all:
43: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx)
44: 	case c.TopoPaths.TopologyFilenameAbsPath() != "":
45: 		containers, err = c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
46: 		if err == nil {
47: 			var tailscaleContainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
48: 			tailscaleContainers, err = c.discoverTailscaleContainers(ctx, opts.nodeFilter)
49: 			containers = append(containers, tailscaleContainers...)
50: 		}
51: 	default:
52: 		var listOpts []ListOption
53: 
54: 		if c.Config.Name != "" {
55: 			listOpts = []ListOption{WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)}
56: 		} else {
57: 			listOpts = []ListOption{WithListclabLabelExists()}
58: 		}
59: 
60: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx, listOpts...)
61: 	}
62: 
63: 	if err != nil {
64: 		return err
65: 	}
66: 
67: 	// topo will hold the reference to the topology file
68: 	// as the key and the respective lab directory as the referenced value
69: 	topos := map[string]string{}
70: 
71: 	for idx := range containers {
72: 		topoFile, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
73: 		if !ok {
74: 			continue
75: 		}
76: 
77: 		topos[topoFile] = filepath.Dir(containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeLabDir])
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	// failedLabDirs records the lab directories whose destroy failed or was never
81: 	// attempted. --cleanup keeps those directories: deleting the lab directory of
82: 	// a lab that is still running would discard the state file destroy needs for
83: 	// its management network cleanup on the next attempt.
84: 	failedLabDirs := map[string]bool{}

86: 	defer func() {

88: 			if cleanupErr := c.destroyLabDirs(topos, opts.all, failedLabDirs); cleanupErr != nil {
89: 				err = errors.Join(err, cleanupErr)
90: 			}
91: 		}
92: 	}()
93: 
94: 	if len(topos) == 0 {
95: 		return nil
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	log.Debugf("got the following topologies for destroy: %+v", topos)
99: 
100: 	// if all, and cli doesn't have --yes flag, and in a terminal -- prompt user confirmation
101: 	if opts.all && opts.terminalPrompt && term.IsTerminal(int(os.Stdin.Fd())) {
102: 		err := cliPromptToDestroyAll(topos)
103: 		if err != nil {
104: 			return err
105: 		}
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	var errs []error
109: 
110: 	for topo, labDir := range topos {
111: 		cc, err := c.makeCopyForDestroy(ctx, topo, labDir, *opts)
112: 		if err != nil {
113: 			log.Errorf("error creating clab instance for topo %q: %v", topo, err)
114: 
115: 			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf(
116: 				"error creating clab instance for topo %q: %w", topo, err))
117: 			failedLabDirs[labDir] = true
118: 
119: 			continue
120: 		}
121: 
122: 		err = cc.destroy(ctx, opts.maxWorkers, opts.keepMgmtNet)
123: 		if err != nil {
124: 			log.Errorf("Error occurred during the %s lab deletion: %v", cc.Config.Name, err)
125: 			errs = append(errs, err)
126: 			failedLabDirs[labDir] = true
127: 		}
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	if len(errs) != 0 {
131: 		return fmt.Errorf("error(s) occurred during the deletion. Check log messages")
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	return nil
135: }
136: 
137: // creates a mostly cloned version of the current c but set to the new topology, and with the
138: // necessary steps (mgmt network things) handled preparing the new CLab for destruction.
139: func (c *CLab) makeCopyForDestroy(
140: 	ctx context.Context,
141: 	topo,
142: 	labDir string,
143: 	opts DestroyOptions,
144: ) (*CLab, error) {
145: 	newOpts := []ClabOption{
146: 		WithTimeout(c.timeout),
147: 	}
148: 
149: 	// Try to load topology file if it exists, otherwise use lab name only.
150: 	// This allows destroying labs even when the topology file has been deleted.
151: 	// WithTopoPath / WithLabNameOnly must run before WithNodeFilter so filterClabNodes
152: 	// sees topology nodes (see filterClabNodes in clab.go).
153: 	varsFiles := opts.varsFiles
154: 	if len(varsFiles) == 0 {
155: 		varsFiles = c.TopoPaths.VarsFilenamesAbsPath()
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	if clabutils.FileOrDirExists(topo) {
159: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithTopoPath(topo, varsFiles))
160: 	} else {
161: 		// Derive lab name from lab directory (format: clab-<labname>)
162: 		labName := filepath.Base(labDir)
163: 		labName = strings.TrimPrefix(labName, "clab-")
164: 		log.Debugf("topology file %q not found, using lab name %q for destroy", topo, labName)
165: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithLabNameOnly(labName))
166: 	}
167: 
168: 	newOpts = append(newOpts,
169: 		WithNodeFilter(opts.nodeFilter),
170: 		// during destroy we don't want to check bind paths
171: 		// as it is irrelevant for this command.
172: 		WithSkippedBindsPathsCheck(),
173: 		WithRuntime(
174: 			c.globalRuntimeName,
175: 			&clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{
176: 				Debug:            c.Config.Debug,
177: 				Timeout:          c.timeout,
178: 				GracefulShutdown: opts.graceful,
179: 			},
180: 		),
181: 	)
182: 
183: 	if opts.keepMgmtNet {
184: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithKeepMgmtNet())
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	cc, err := NewContainerLab(newOpts...)
188: 	if err != nil {
189: 		return nil, err
190: 	}
191: 	if labDir != "" && clabutils.FileOrDirExists(labDir) {
192: 		// adjust the labdir. Usually we take the PWD. but now on destroy time,
193: 		// we might be in a different Dir.
194: 		err = cc.TopoPaths.SetLabDir(labDir)
195: 		if err != nil {
196: 			return nil, err
197: 		}
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	err = clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(cc.Config.Mgmt.Bridge)
201: 	if err != nil {
202: 		return nil, err
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	// populate the management network state (bridge names) destroy needs for the
206: 	// removal of the iptables rules, without creating networks that are missing:
207: 	// a lab deployed with a management network override leaves the topology's
208: 	// default network uncreated, and creating it here would collide with the
209: 	// subnets the override is using.
210: 	if !cc.mgmtNetworksSkipped() {
211: 		var bridged clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
212: 		for _, m := range cc.allMgmtNetworks() {
213: 			if m.Driver != clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
214: 				bridged = append(bridged, m)
215: 			}
216: 		}
217: 		if err = cc.resolveMgmtNetworksForDestroy(ctx, bridged); err != nil {
218: 			return nil, err
219: 		}
220: 	}
221: 
222: 	err = cc.ResolveLinks()
223: 	if err != nil {
224: 		return nil, err
225: 	}
226: 
227: 	return cc, nil
228: }
229: 
230: // resolveMgmtNetworksForDestroy inspects the management networks that already exist
231: // so their bridge names are populated for the iptables cleanup. Networks that do not
232: // exist are skipped: they hold no bridge to clean up, and destroy must not create
233: // management networks. Runtimes that cannot check network existence keep the
234: // create-or-inspect behavior.
235: func (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetworksForDestroy(
236: 	ctx context.Context,
237: 	networks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks,
238: ) error {
239: 	var existing clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
240: 
241: 	for _, m := range networks {
242: 		rt, rtErr := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
243: 		if rtErr != nil {
244: 			// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks
245: 			log.Debug(
246: 				"Skipping management network resolution", "network", m.Network, "error", rtErr,
247: 			)
248: 			continue
249: 		}
250: 
251: 		checker, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtNetworkChecker)
252: 		if !ok {
253: 			existing = append(existing, m)
254: 			continue
255: 		}
256: 
257: 		exists, err := checker.MgmtNetworkExists(ctx)
258: 		if err != nil {
259: 			return err
260: 		}
261: 		if !exists {
262: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network that does not exist", "network", m.Network)
263: 			continue
264: 		}
265: 
266: 		existing = append(existing, m)
267: 	}
268: 
269: 	return c.createNetworks(ctx, existing)
270: }
271: 
272: // destroyLabDirs removes the lab directories of the destroyed labs. Directories
273: // recorded in failedLabDirs are kept: their labs are still running, and the
274: // directory holds the lab state a later destroy attempt relies on.
275: func (c *CLab) destroyLabDirs(
276: 	topos map[string]string,
277: 	all bool,
278: 	failedLabDirs map[string]bool,
279: ) error {
280: 	if len(topos) == 0 {
281: 		log.Info("no containerlab containers found")
282: 
283: 		if !all {
284: 			labDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()
285: 			if labDir == "" {
286: 				// Try to infer the precise lab directory when TopologyLabDir is not set,
287: 				// e.g. destroy --name without topology file. Only look for an exact
288: 				// clab-<name> match, never a wildcard.
289: 				if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
290: 					candidates := []string{}
291: 					if base := os.Getenv("CLAB_LABDIR_BASE"); base != "" {
292: 						candidates = append(candidates, filepath.Join(base, "clab-"+c.Config.Name))
293: 					}
294: 					if c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileIsSet() {
295: 						candidates = append(
296: 							candidates,
297: 							filepath.Join(c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir(), "clab-"+c.Config.Name),
298: 						)
299: 					} else {
300: 						if wd, err := os.Getwd(); err == nil && wd != "" {
301: 							candidates = append(
302: 								candidates,
303: 								filepath.Join(wd, "clab-"+c.Config.Name),
304: 							)
305: 						}
306: 					}
307: 					for _, cand := range candidates {
308: 						if _, err := os.Stat(cand); err == nil {
309: 							labDir = cand
310: 							break
311: 						}
312: 					}
313: 					if labDir == "" {
314: 						log.Info(
315: 							"lab directory not found, nothing to clean",
316: 							"candidates",
317: 							candidates,
318: 						)
319: 						return nil
320: 					}
321: 				} else {
322: 					log.Info("lab directory not found, nothing to clean")
323: 					return nil
324: 				}
325: 			}
326: 
327: 			// Safety: only remove directories that look like a lab directory.
328: 			base := filepath.Base(labDir)
329: 			if !strings.HasPrefix(base, "clab-") {
330: 				log.Warnf("refusing to remove lab directory %q: unexpected name", labDir)
331: 				return nil
332: 			}
333: 			if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
334: 				expected := "clab-" + c.Config.Name
335: 				if base != expected {
336: 					log.Warnf(
337: 						"refusing to remove lab directory %q: does not match expected %q",
338: 						labDir,
339: 						expected,
340: 					)
341: 					return nil
342: 				}
343: 			}
344: 
345: 			if _, err := os.Stat(labDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
346: 				log.Info("lab directory not found, nothing to clean", "path", labDir)
347: 				return nil
348: 			} else if err != nil {
349: 				log.Debug("failed to stat lab directory, skipping", "path", labDir, "error", err)
350: 				return nil
351: 			}
352: 
353: 			log.Info("Removing lab directory", "path", labDir)
354: 			if err := os.RemoveAll(labDir); err != nil {
355: 				log.Errorf("error deleting lab directory: %v", err)
356: 				return err
357: 			}
358: 		}
359: 
360: 		return nil
361: 	}
362: 
363: 	for _, labdir := range topos {
364: 		if failedLabDirs[labdir] {
365: 			log.Warnf(
366: 				"keeping lab directory %s: the lab was not destroyed, so its state is kept for a later attempt",
367: 				labdir,
368: 			)
369: 
370: 			continue
371: 		}
372: 
373: 		err := os.RemoveAll(labdir)
374: 		if err != nil {
375: 			log.Errorf("error deleting lab directory: %v", err)
376: 		}
377: 	}
378: 
379: 	return nil
380: }
381: 
382: func (c *CLab) destroy(ctx context.Context, maxWorkers uint, keepMgmtNet bool) error {
383: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
384: 	var orphanSidecars []clabruntime.GenericContainer

Networks that do not\n// exist are skipped: they hold no bridge to clean up, and destroy must not create\n// management networks. Runtimes that cannot check network existence keep the\n// create-or-inspect behavior.\nfunc (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetworksForDestroy(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tnetworks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks,\n) error {\n\tvar existing clabtypes.MgmtNetworks\n\n\tfor _, m := range networks {\n\t\trt, rtErr := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)\n\t\tif rtErr != nil {\n\t\t\t// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks\n\t\t\tlog.Debug(\n\t\t\t\t\"Skipping management network resolution\", \"network\", m.Network, \"error\", rtErr,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tchecker, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtNetworkChecker)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\texisting = append(existing, m)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\texists, err := checker.MgmtNetworkExists(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !exists {\n\t\t\tlog.Debug(\"Skipping management network that does not exist\", \"network\", m.Network)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\texisting = append(existing, m)\n\t}\n\n\treturn c.createNetworks(ctx, existing)\n}\n\n// destroyLabDirs removes the lab directories of the destroyed labs. Reasoning
Now I understand the destroy-by-name-only flow: `makeCopyForDestroy` without a topo file → `WithLabNameOnly` → c.Nodes empty → `deleteContainersDirect(ctx, containers)` where containers come from `ListContainers(WithListLabName(...))`.

Now the key question for correctness of the added symlink deletion: the docker runtime's `Names[0]` includes a leading "/" (e.g., "/clab-mgmt13-n1"), so `DeleteNetnsSymlink("/clab-mgmt13-n1")` produces "/run/netns//clab-mgmt13-n1". On Linux this resolves to "/run/netns/clab-mgmt13-n1", so `os.Remove` works. Is this also true for podman? Podman `v.Names` — podman ps JSON "Names" is an array without leading slash. So fine.

But wait — the test suite runs with docker only (Skip If runtime != docker). And the double slash works. Hmm, but is the leading slash really there? Let me verify in the docker runtime ListContainers, how `names` is built (line ~1541).

Reasoning
Good: docker runtime trims the leading "/" when producing GenericContainer.Names. So `name` in `deleteContainersDirect` is "clab-mgmt13-n1" — matches the symlink name (LongName). 

Now, `deleteToolContainers` at line 773 also trims the prefix before use — consistent.

Now check podman: line 459 `Names: v.Names`. Podman ps --format json "Names" — podman's libpod containers list JSON has "Names": ["name"] without leading slash. Fine.

Now, is there a subtle issue: `DeleteNetnsSymlink` uses `os.Remove` on the path. If the symlink doesn't exist, error logged at debug. Fine, error ignored with `_ =` — consistent with line 616.

Now the tail of the destroy function after deleteContainersDirect: for the destroy-by-name-only case, c.Nodes is empty, so the loop at 466-471 does nothing. Good — this is the bug the PR fixes.

But wait — there's another subtlety: in the destroy-by-name-only path, `deleteContainersDirect` also gets called at line 444 for orphanSidecars when nodes exist. Orphan sidecar tailscale containers (ToolType == ""). Do tailscale containers get netns symlinks? Tailscale node kind — DefaultNode? Tailscale node's DeleteNetnsSymlink? The tailscale node probably inherits DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink which deletes by container name. And orphan sidecars are containers with the lab label but no matching node in c.Nodes. Adding symlink deletion for them is consistent.

Now check `logoutTailscaleContainer` — pre-existing.

Another check: `deleteToolContainers` doesn't delete netns symlinks for tool containers, but tool containers (sshx/gotty) don't have symlinks (they're created via a different path — probably without postStartActions). Now the test: "Netns symlinks are created for both nodes" runs after deploy. Deploy creates symlink in postStartActions with LongName "clab-mgmt13-n1". Good.

Then makeCopyForDestroy: topo file doesn't exist → WithLabNameOnly → cc has no nodes → cc.destroy → deleteContainersDirect(cc containers) → now deletes symlinks. 

Then cc.destroyLabDirs — the deferred cleanup removes the lab dir. Fine.

Now — one question: does `--cleanup` in destroy remove the lab directory which... irrelevant to symlinks.

Potential issue 1: the `continue` addition means symlink deletion skipped on failed delete — correct behavior (you don't want to remove the netns symlink of a container that failed to be removed... actually, hmm, if DeleteContainer failed, the container still exists, and its netns symlink points to a live netns; removing it would break iproute2 management. So skipping is right).

Potential issue 2: What about podman? Podman Names might contain leading slash? Let me check podman list code around line 459.

Let me also double check `DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink` uses `OverwriteNode.GetContainerName()` — LongName. And deploy creates via postStartActions with node.LongName. Consistent.

Now let's also check: in the destroy-with-topo path (c.Nodes > 0), node.DeleteNetnsSymlink() is called for all nodes at 466. And orphanSidecars go through deleteContainersDirect. If an orphanSidecar is also in c.Nodes... it's excluded (`if _, desired := c.Nodes[...]; desired { continue }`). So no double removal. Good.

But hmm — is there a double-deletion scenario? In destroy-by-name-only, deleteContainersDirect deletes symlink right after container deletion. Then the loop over c.Nodes is empty. Fine.

Now what about the case where c.Nodes > 0 (topo present) and containers are deleted by deleteNodes (node.Delete), and the symlinks are removed later at line 466. Orphan sidecars via deleteContainersDirect — new symlink removal. No conflict.

Potential issue 3: `deleteContainersDirect` is also invoked from tests — tailscale_test.go line 692. Let me check that test to ensure the mock runtime's DeleteContainer success path now triggers DeleteNetnsSymlink — which touches /run/netns. In tests running as non-root in CI, os.Remove on /run/netns/clab-mylab-n1-ts would fail (permission denied) — but the error is ignored (`_ =`). DeleteNetnsSymlink returns nil always anyway. Fine.

Let me look at that test quickly to be thorough.

Is that a real problem? Leftover symlinks in /run/netns pointing to deleted namespaces. The PR improves but doesn't fully fix for sros. Is this a finding? It's a partial fix; the maintainer may accept it. But should I flag it? The test only covers two plain linux nodes. It's a gap but the PR's goal is presumably to fix the leak for the common case. Reasoning
Now, one more consideration: is there a stale-netns-symlink cleanup for symlinks whose names don't match a container in the destroy-by-name-only path — no. Fine.

Another point: `deleteContainersDirect` is called for `orphanSidecars` when `len(c.Nodes) > 0`. Orphan sidecars are tailscale-discovered containers NOT present as nodes. These get symlink removal now — good.

Now let me consider the `continue` semantics vs the previous behavior more carefully:

Base:
```go
err := rt.DeleteContainer(ctx, name)
if err != nil {
    log.Errorf(...)
}
// end of loop iteration
```
Head:
```go
err := rt.DeleteContainer(ctx, name)
if err != nil {
    log.Errorf(...)
    continue
}
_ = clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(name)
```
Equivalent to if/else. Fine, though `continue` at the end of an if branch before trailing code is idiomatic. OK.

Now — is there any concern about `logoutTailscaleContainer` failing and DeleteContainer not being called? No, logout is best-effort.

Now, the robot test. In the test, the "Netns symlinks are created for both nodes" keyword runs right after deploy. Fine.

One thing to check: "Command Should Fail    test -L /run/netns/clab-mgmt13-n1". If the symlink removal fails silently (error ignored) in Go code, the test would catch it. Good — test covers the fix.

Now — a question: does `destroy --cleanup` also remove the lab directory that holds the state? Yes, deferred. Symlinks in /run/netns are outside labdir.

Potential issue: The symlink deletion happens only when `rt.DeleteContainer` succeeds. If the container is already gone (NotFound error), DeleteContainer returns error → symlink NOT deleted → stale symlink remains. Hmm. In the destroy-by-name-only path, containers are listed just before, so they exist. But in a race (container deleted concurrently, e.g., by another clab process or manually), DeleteContainer may return NotFound and the symlink remains. Is DeleteContainer idempotent? Let me check docker's DeleteContainer — does it treat NotFound as success? Let me check.

590: 	force := !r.config.GracefulShutdown

592: 	if err != nil {

But there's another scenario: in the destroy-with-nodes path (line 444), `orphanSidecars` come from `discoverTailscaleContainers` — also listed just before. Tiny race.

95: 	return moved, nil
96: }
97: 
98: func (p *ParkingNode) DiscoverOwnedEndpoints(ctx context.Context, original Node) error {
99: 	ifaceNames, err := listOwnedInterfaceNames(
100: 		ctx,
101: 		p,
102: 		trackedIfaceNames(original.GetEndpoints(), p.GetEndpoints()),
103: 	)
104: 	if err != nil {
105: 		return err
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	discovered := make(map[string]struct{}, len(ifaceNames))
109: 	for _, ifaceName := range ifaceNames {
110: 		discovered[ifaceName] = struct{}{}
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), p.GetEndpoints()...) {
114: 		if _, exists := discovered[ep.GetIfaceName()]; exists {
115: 			continue
116: 		}
117: 
118: 		if err := p.ReleaseEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
119: 			return err
120: 		}
121: 		if ep.IsRuntimeDiscovered() {
122: 			continue
123: 		}
124: 
125: 		ep.SetNode(original)
126: 		if err := original.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
127: 			return err
128: 		}
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	known := make(map[string]Endpoint, len(original.GetEndpoints())+len(p.GetEndpoints()))
132: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), original.GetEndpoints()...) {
133: 		known[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
134: 	}
135: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), p.GetEndpoints()...) {
136: 		known[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	for _, ifaceName := range ifaceNames {
140: 		if ep, ok := known[ifaceName]; ok {
141: 			if ep.GetNode() == Node(p) {
142: 				continue
143: 			}
144: 
145: 			if err := ep.GetNode().ReleaseEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
146: 				return err
147: 			}
148: 			ep.SetNode(p)
149: 			if err := p.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
150: 				return err
151: 			}
152: 			continue
153: 		}
154: 
155: 		runtimeEp := NewRuntimeEndpoint(p, ifaceName)
156: 		if err := p.AdoptEndpoint(runtimeEp); err != nil {
157: 			return err
158: 		}
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	return nil
162: }
163: 
164: func (p *ParkingNode) captureCandidates(
165: 	ctx context.Context,
166: 	src Node,
167: ) ([]Endpoint, error) {
168: 	trackedEndpoints := src.GetEndpoints()
169: 	tracked := make(map[string]Endpoint, len(trackedEndpoints))
170: 	for _, ep := range trackedEndpoints {
171: 		tracked[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
172: 	}
173: 	presentIfaceNames, err := listOwnedInterfaceNames(ctx, src, trackedIfaceNames(trackedEndpoints))
174: 	if err != nil {
175: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
176: 			"failed to discover runtime interfaces for node %q: %w",
177: 			src.GetShortName(),
178: 			err,
179: 		)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	endpoints := make([]Endpoint, 0, len(presentIfaceNames))
183: 	for _, ifaceName := range presentIfaceNames {
184: 		ep, ok := tracked[ifaceName]
185: 		if !ok {
186: 			ep = NewRuntimeEndpoint(src, ifaceName)
187: 			if err := src.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
188: 				return nil, err
189: 			}
190: 		}
191: 
192: 		endpoints = append(endpoints, ep)
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	return endpoints, nil
196: }
197: 
198: func trackedIfaceNames(endpointSets ...[]Endpoint) map[string]struct{} {
199: 	ifaceNames := map[string]struct{}{}
200: 
201: 	for _, endpoints := range endpointSets {
202: 		for _, ep := range endpoints {
203: 			ifaceNames[ep.GetIfaceName()] = struct{}{}
204: 		}
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	return ifaceNames
208: }
209: 
210: func listOwnedInterfaceNames(
211: 	ctx context.Context,
212: 	node Node,
213: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
214: ) ([]string, error) {
215: 	var ifaces []string
216: 
217: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
218: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
219: 		if err != nil {
220: 			return err
221: 		}
222: 
223: 		for _, link := range links {
224: 			if !isOwnedInterface(link, knownIfaceNames) {
225: 				continue
226: 			}
227: 
228: 			ifaces = append(ifaces, link.Attrs().Name)
229: 		}
230: 
231: 		return nil
232: 	})
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	slices.Sort(ifaces)
238: 
239: 	return ifaces, nil
240: }
241: 
242: func isOwnedInterface(link netlink.Link, knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{}) bool {
243: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
244: 	switch name {
245: 	case "lo", "eth0":
246: 		return false
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known && !hasOwnershipAltName(link) {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	return true
254: }
255: 
256: func (*ParkingNode) GetLinkEndpointType() LinkEndpointType {
257: 	return LinkEndpointTypeVeth
258: }

(End of file - total 258 lines)

# PR Review: Remove netns symlinks in the destroy-by-name path

## Summary

The PR fixes a leak of `/run/netns/<container-name>` symlinks in the destroy-by-name-only flow (`destroy --name ... --cleanup` after the topology file is gone). Previously, `deleteContainersDirect` removed containers via the runtime API but never deleted the netns symlink that `LinkContainerNS` creates at deploy time, because the symlink cleanup loop at `core/destroy.go:466-471` only iterates over `c.Nodes`, which is empty in the name-only flow. The change also adds an integration test asserting both symlink creation and removal.

The fix is small, targeted, and correct for the covered case. The symlink name matches: docker's `produceGenericContainerList` trims the leading `/` from `Names` (`runtime/docker/docker.go:1534-1538`), podman reports names without the slash (`runtime/podman/util.go:459`), and `LinkContainerNS` creates the link under the node's `LongName` (`runtime/docker/docker.go:1421`, `runtime/podman/util.go:751`), so `DeleteNetnsSymlink(name)` resolves to the exact path created at deploy time. Adding `continue` on delete failure is also right: removing the symlink of a container whose removal failed would break `ip netns` management for a container that is still alive.

## Findings

| Priority | Title |
|----------|-------|
| P4 | `deleteContainersDirect` skips symlink cleanup on `NotFound`, leaving the exact leak this PR fixes in a race window |

<details>
<summary><strong>P4: <code>deleteContainersDirect</code> skips symlink cleanup on <code>NotFound</code>, leaving the exact leak this PR fixes in a race window</strong></summary>

**Location:** [core/destroy.go L725-L731 (head)](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0b29d0fb597b30f4254061a67f6a29cc3a5951fb/core/destroy.go#L725-L731)

**Problem:** The new code only calls `clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(name)` when `rt.DeleteContainer` succeeds. Both `DockerRuntime.DeleteContainer` (`runtime/docker/docker.go:1748-1754`) and `PodmanRuntime.DeleteContainer` (`runtime/podman/podman.go:605-610`) propagate a not-found error when the container is already gone, so in that case the `continue` skips symlink removal and the stale `/run/netns/<name>` symlink — precisely what this PR sets out to clean — survives.

**Trigger:** A container vanishes between `ListContainers` and `DeleteContainer`, e.g. a concurrent `destroy` (including `destroy --all` racing itself) or an operator removing the container manually. `DockerRuntime.produceGenericContainerList` even documents this race (`runtime/docker/docker.go:1562-1564`), so it is a recognized window.

**Impact:** Minor: one stale symlink per raced container. It self-heals when a same-named container is redeployed (`LinkContainerNS` removes an existing destination before symlinking, `utils/netlink.go:60-63`), and destroy already logs the removal error, so nothing silently pretends full success. It is also inconsistent with the node-based path, where the symlink loop at `core/destroy.go:466-471` removes symlinks regardless of `node.Delete` errors.

**Suggestion:** The simplest consistent correction is to attempt symlink removal after every delete attempt in `deleteContainersDirect` (mirroring the node-based path), accepting that a failed non-race delete leaves a live container with no symlink; alternatively, keep the guard but let the not-found error fall through to symlink removal. Either way, since this is a narrow race with a self-healing outcome, treating it as an optional improvement is appropriate.

</details>

## Coverage notes

- Both changed paths were fully traced: the `destroy --name` (no topo) flow reaching `deleteContainersDirect` with an empty `c.Nodes` via `WithLabNameOnly`/`makeCopyForDestroy`, and the `orphanSidecars` call site when nodes exist. The name-only case is the one the fix targets, and the orphan-sidecar case benefits consistently.
- Symlink naming was verified end to end: created with the node `LongName` in both docker and podman `postStartActions`, deleted with the trimmed `GenericContainer.Names[0]` in this PR, and the existing sibling patterns (`deleteApplyNodes` at `core/destroy.go:616`, node loop at `core/destroy.go:466-471`) were compared.
- The Robot test additions were checked against the shared keywords in `tests/15-mgmt/mgmt.resource` (`Command Should Succeed`/`Command Should Fail`) and the topology fixture (`13-destroy-without-topo.clab.yml` uses linux nodes `n1`/`n2`, matching the asserted `clab-mgmt13-n1`/`clab-mgmt13-n2` long names).
- Known gaps that are pre-existing and not worsened by this PR were deliberately not raised as findings: multi-netns node kinds (e.g. Nokia SR OS component/netns/parking netns entries, `nodes/sros/netns.go:139-156`) still leave extra `/run/netns` entries in the name-only path, and `deleteToolContainers` never removed symlinks. Both were unhandled before this change.
- This is a source-only review of the snapshot; no tests or builds were executed. The Robot suites are the executable check for this change and are wired into the mgmt CI workflow.
- No schema impact: no topology or configuration API surface changed. No user-facing docs claim destruction of `/run/netns` entries, so no documentation update is required.
